# The Health walk in Welsh — 438 turns, the pair overlay

**Authored 2026-09-02. THIS IS THE REAL THING, NOT A DRAFT.** All 438 Welsh lines are live in
`canonical_pod_scenarios` under pod slug `health`, `target_lang = cym_n`, visible and editable in
the Script Lab's three-column view at `popty.app/canonical/scripts/health`. Nothing here is
awaiting approval, nothing is held back, and no line waits on this document. Aran is the language
authority and the correction loop: he edits in place, and every edit he makes is one click and is
already versioned by the lab's own save route.

**What this is.** The Welsh target side of the health sector walk — 23 contexts, 73 flows,
438 turns of hand-authored English by Aran, the exemplar every other themed walk is written
against. This is **frame-mapping, not translation**: the English known side owns the frames, and
every chunk mapping below is classified **deterministic / split / inversion / erasure**, every
re-cut is reported, and the places where the curriculum relocates for Welsh are named.

**The English canon was not touched.** Not in the markdown, not in the database, not by re-cutting,
not by fixing anything that looked like a typo. Verified mechanically after every write: all 438
`english_text` values are byte-identical to `docs/sector-pods/source/health-sector-conversations-v3.md`.

**Dialect: `cym_n` — northern colloquial Welsh, Aran's own**, binding to the base course
`cym_n_for_eng` and consistent with the 57-row general-role cut already live under
`health-general-welsh`. **One canon, annotated for the south** — §5. Not one turn in the 438 needs
a different southern *sentence*; 21 need a re-ordered southern *line*, and every one of those is
the same `gen i` fork.

**ZUT, one-directional, and the sweep is clean.** One English intention → exactly one Welsh form,
corpus-wide. Convergence — several English knowns landing on one Welsh form — is reception, and it
is welcome; 28 of them are listed in §3. Divergence kills. All 438 turns were swept end to end for
same-known → two-targets; **four forks came back, all four were resolved by re-cutting the unit
rather than blocking a word, and the re-sweep came back clean at zero.** §3 has every one.

**Authoring only.** No voice was cast, no audio was generated, and nothing entered a recording
queue. Welsh is the estate's human-recorded language: text is editable in place for nothing, and a
wrong line that has been to studio costs studio time and pulls a human into a booth. So the ship-
as-real doctrine stops exactly here.

---

## 0. Standing decisions

**R1 — `chi` throughout.** The standing law is tu-first *unless context insists*, and a clinician
addressing a patient is the context that insists: every one of these 438 turns is duty-of-care
speech between a professional and a stranger, mostly an elderly one, and ZUT wants one you-form
corpus-wide anyway. The patient's own speech to the worker is `chi` too — in this corpus the
patients call the nurse *bach*, *del* and *love*, which is warmth, not intimacy of address. No turn
in the corpus signalled a genuine `ti`.

**R2 — the deixis lands where the fiction puts it.** The 57-seed cut had generic deixis ("this
isn't my first language") and resolved it to *Cymraeg*. This corpus is specific: the Filipino nurse
and doctor say "English isn't my first language", so the Welsh says *dim Saesneg ydy fy iaith gynta
i*. See the note on the English-version flows in §7.

**R3 — the frame ledger.** One Welsh frame per English frame, applied without exception. Frames
marked *(57)* are reused from the general-role cut rather than re-invented.

| English frame | Welsh frame | contexts |
|---|---|---|
| "shall we X?" / "shall I X?" | *wnawn ni X?* / *wna i X?* *(57)* | 1.1, 1.7, 1.8, 1.2 |
| "let's X" | *dewch i ni X* *(57)* | 1.0, 1.2, 1.3, 1.4, 1.9, 2.2, 2.3, 2.4, 2.9, 2.10 |
| "let me X" | *gadwch i mi X* *(57)* | 1.3, 1.4, 1.5, 1.7, 1.9, 2.1, 2.2, 2.3, 2.5 |
| "would you like…?" | *fasach chi'n licio…?* / *liciech chi…?* *(57)* | 1.1, 1.4, 1.7 |
| "that's what X is for" / "what's X for?" | *dyna i be mae X* / *i be mae X?* *(57)* | 1.2, 1.5, 1.9 |
| "X it is" (the ratifier) | *X amdani* *(57)* | 1.0, 1.7, 1.11, 2.0, 2.10 |
| "I'm just going to X" (announce-then-act) | *dw i jyst yn mynd i X* *(57)* | 1.3, 2.4 |
| "all done" / "there we are" / "for now" | *wedi gorffen* / *dyna ni* / *am rŵan* *(57)* | 1.3, 1.6 |
| declarative future "we'll X / I'll X" | *mi* + short future *(57)* | pervasive |
| need-family | *angen* (*dw i angen / dach chi angen / dim angen*) *(57)* | pervasive |
| obligation "you'll have to / I might need to" | *bydd raid i X* | 1.3, 1.10, 1.0 |
| epistemic "might / maybe / probably" | *ella* + plain tense | 1.0, 1.2, 1.6, 1.9, 2.2 |
| "just" as imperative softener | **erased** *(57)* | pervasive |
| "just" elsewhere (loose) | *jyst* *(57)* | pervasive |
| "just" as restrictive "only" | *dim ond* | 1.5, 1.6, 2.3, 2.10 |
| "just" as precise "exactly there" | *yn union* | 2.4, 2.10 |
| "keep -ing" / "still -ing" | *dal i* + verbal noun | 1.3, 1.5, 2.6, 2.7, 2.9 |
| "tell me" | *deudwch wrtha i* (governs *wrth*) *(57)* | pervasive |
| "let me know" | *rhowch wybod i mi* *(57)* — kept apart from "tell me" | 1.0, 1.7 |
| "manage" | *dod i ben* | 1.0, 1.3, 1.8, 2.7 |
| "cope" | *ymdopi* — kept apart from "manage" | 2.5 |
| "bring X" / "take X" | *dod â X* / *mynd â X* | 1.2, 1.4, 1.10, 2.0, 2.1 |
| "call" (summon a person) | *galw ar* *(57)* | 1.0, 2.0 |
| "call in / call round" | *galw heibio* — the particle changes the sense entirely | 2.7 |
| "ring" (a bell) / "ring" (a phone) | *canu'r gloch* / *ffonio* — two verbs, never one | 1.9, 1.11, 2.5, 2.8 |
| "go wrong" | *mynd o'i le* | 1.11 |
| alternative question "X or Y?" | *X 'ta Y?* | 1.7, 2.2, 2.4, 2.6 |
| "before X" / "while X" / "so that X" | *cyn i X* / *tra X* / *fel bod X* *(57)* | pervasive |
| "I'd rather / I'm sorry / I'm pleased / I'm glad" | *mae'n well / mae'n ddrwg / mae'n dda gen i* | 1.0, 1.7, 1.9, 2.0, 2.6, 2.9 |
| possession "I've got / you've got / the doctor will have" | *mae … gen i / gynnoch chi / gan y doctor* | pervasive |
| "X away" (ask away, ring away) | *X faint fynnoch chi* *(57)* | 1.0, 1.9 |
| "off we go / off you go / fire away" | *ffwrdd â ni / â chi* *(57)* | 1.0, 1.8, 1.11 |
| the ratifying "Right," opening a move | *Reit* | pervasive |
| "Righto" / "Right you are" | *Reit-o* / *O'r gorau* — three knowns, three forms | pervasive |
| "Understood" / "Got it" | *Dallt* | 1.5, 1.10, 1.11, 2.6, 2.8 |

**R4 — the pace family, four knowns, four forms** *(57)*: "slowly" → *yn ara bach*; "nice and slow"
→ *yn ara deg*; "extra slow" → *yn ara deg iawn*; "take it steady" → *mynd gan bwyll*. And "nice and
clear" is **not** in this family — it takes *yn braf a chlir*, because it is a different known.

**R5 — numbers are modern decimal** *(57)*. Blood pressure is read digit-group-wise ("135 dros 80");
years are read as whole numbers (*mil naw pedwar deg un*); the blood-sugar figures in 2.9 are
spelled out (*pedwar deg wyth*) so they read aloud correctly rather than as digits a reader has to
convert mid-sentence.

**R6 — borrowings are register, not laziness** *(57)*. *tsiecio, sortio, jobsys, sbwnj, cwstard,
jwg, plîs, jyst, lot, saff, neis, reit, sori, niwsans, ypsetio, tecstio, ffonio, bwcio* are how the
ward actually talks in the north, and the corpus's own patients say "bach" and "Righto".

**R7 — one lexeme per production family** *(57)*: check → *tsiec-* everywhere; the bell → *y gloch*;
use → *defnyddio*; visiting → *yr oriau ymweld*; kind → *clên*; sensible → *call*; trouble/bother →
*traffarth*; offence → *digio*; strange/unfamiliar → *diarth*; dairy → *llaeth*; approval →
*campus* (which "Grand" and "Champion" both land on).

**R8 — grammatical gender rulings** *(57, extended)*: *poen* **feminine** (*y boen*, *mae hi'n
waeth*); *cloch* feminine; *braich*, *brest*, *tabled*, *potel*, *taflen*, *rhestr*, *canmoliaeth*,
*her*, *calon*, *anadl* feminine; the blood-pressure reading and the cough masculine *o*. The
ambient "it" of weather and noise is **feminine** *hi* (*ydy hi'n swnllyd?*), which English's
neuter gives no clue about.

**R9 — mutation is absorbed, never taught.** Every initial mutation in this corpus lives inside a
chunk — *fy nallt i*, *y ddwy wen*, *tri pheth*, *y frest*, *dwy botel fach*. A learner meets them
as worn phrases; the overlay never lifts one out as an item.

---
## 1. The mapping — 23 contexts, 73 flows, 438 turns, chunk by chunk

Format per turn: the **canonical English verbatim** — never edited, never re-cut, never
normalised — then the Welsh line, then each cut unit → its Welsh form → its class → what the
mapping did. Classes: **D** deterministic · **S** split (see §3) · **I** inversion · **E** erasure.
**⚠** marks a turn inside a flow the corpus tags safety-critical; those take the plainest
register in the walk. `S` after a chunk means the southern form differs there — collected in §5.


### Context 1.0 — Linguistic situation opener (nurse)


**Flow 1**

**SC01-F01-S01** HW — "I'm learning Welsh at the moment, so I'll try to stay in Welsh as much as possible, but if we get stuck I might need to ask you to speak English, if that's okay. If not, I can call someone who speaks Welsh more confidently than me."
> **Dw i'n dysgu Cymraeg ar y funud, felly mi dria i aros yn y Gymraeg gymaint â phosib — ond os awn ni'n sownd, ella bydd raid i mi ofyn i chi siarad Saesneg, os ydy hynny'n iawn. Os nad ydy o, mi fedra i alw ar rywun sy'n siarad Cymraeg yn fwy hyderus na fi.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| I'm learning Welsh at the moment | dw i'n dysgu Cymraeg ar y funud | D | the walk's opening frame — bare present, no article on the language as object |
| so | felly | D | the so/then fork: felly here, never 'ta (S1) |
| I'll try to stay in Welsh as much as possible | mi dria i aros yn y Gymraeg gymaint â phosib | D | mi + short future; the language takes the article as a location: yn y Gymraeg |
| but if we get stuck | ond os awn ni'n sownd | D | HG03's stuck-idiom reused verbatim — mynd yn sownd |
| I might need to ask you to speak English | ella bydd raid i mi ofyn i chi siarad Saesneg | I | English stacks two modals; Welsh relocates to adverb ella + the impersonal obligation frame bydd raid i mi |
| if that's okay | os ydy hynny'n iawn | D |  |
| If not | Os nad ydy o | D | negative conditional with the dummy o |
| I can call someone who speaks Welsh more confidently than me | mi fedra i alw ar rywun sy'n siarad Cymraeg yn fwy hyderus na fi | I | HG03 verbatim — calling a person takes ar, the summons preposition |

*South:* *mi* → fe / bare · *ella* → falle · *mi fedra i* → fe alla i

**SC01-F01-S02** P — "Chwarae teg i chi! Of course, that's fine. We'll manage together."
> **Chwarae teg i chi! Wrth gwrs, mae hynny'n iawn. Mi ddown ni i ben efo'n gilydd.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Chwarae teg i chi! | Chwarae teg i chi! | D | already Welsh in the canonical English — carried through verbatim, not re-rendered |
| Of course | Wrth gwrs | D |  |
| that's fine | mae hynny'n iawn | D |  |
| We'll manage together | Mi ddown ni i ben efo'n gilydd | I | English intransitive 'manage' relocates to the dod i ben accomplishment idiom |

*South:* *mi* → fe / bare · *efo'n gilydd* → gyda'n gilydd

**SC01-F01-S03** HW — "Diolch, that's very kind. And remember, if anything I say isn't clear, just stop me - any time at all."
> **Diolch, mae hynna'n glên iawn. A chofiwch — os dw i'n deud rhywbeth sydd ddim yn glir, stopiwch fi. Unrhyw bryd o gwbl.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Diolch | Diolch | D | already Welsh in the canonical English — carried verbatim |
| that's very kind | mae hynna'n glên iawn | D | clên is the walk's one kind-lexeme (R7) |
| And remember | A chofiwch | D | -wch imperative, the walk's whole production register |
| if anything I say isn't clear | os dw i'n deud rhywbeth sydd ddim yn glir | I | HG01's inversion: the English relative subject becomes a Welsh object clause — 'if I say something that isn't clear' |
| just stop me | stopiwch fi | E | 'just' as imperative softener erases — the -wch form and intonation carry it (R3) |
| any time at all | unrhyw bryd o gwbl | D |  |

*South:* *glên* → ffeind / caredig · *deud* → dweud · *hynna* → hynny (unreduced)

**SC01-F01-S04** P — "I will, don't you worry. I'm not shy."
> **Mi wna i, peidiwch chi â phoeni. Dw i ddim yn swil.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| I will | Mi wna i | D | the short-future echo answer — Welsh has no bare auxiliary to strand |
| don't you worry | peidiwch chi â phoeni | D | peidiwch â negative imperative; the emphatic chi carries English's 'you' |
| I'm not shy | Dw i ddim yn swil | D |  |

*South:* *mi* → fe / bare

**SC01-F01-S05** HW — "Good. Right then, first things first - I need to take your blood pressure."
> **Da iawn. Reit 'ta — y peth cynta'n gynta: dw i angen cymryd eich pwysau gwaed chi.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Good | Da iawn | D |  |
| Right then | Reit 'ta | D | the in-that-case 'ta, not felly (S1) |
| first things first | y peth cynta'n gynta | E | English's plural idiom singularises; Welsh has no plural reflex here |
| I need to take your blood pressure | dw i angen cymryd eich pwysau gwaed chi | D | HG31 verbatim; the bare angen need-frame (R3) |

*South:* *reit 'ta* → ? · *cynta* → cynta' (reduced differently) · *angen* → mae angen i fi

**SC01-F01-S06** P — "Fire away, bach."
> **Ffwrdd â chi, bach.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Fire away | Ffwrdd â chi | I | the â-propulsion frame (cf. HG48 ffwrdd â ni); English's firearm metaphor has no Welsh reflex |
| bach | bach | D | already Welsh in the canonical English — the corpus's own endearment, carried verbatim |


**Flow 2**

**SC01-F02-S01** HW — "I'm learning Welsh at the moment, so I'll try to stay in Welsh as much as I can, but I might need to ask for help now and then, if that's alright."
> **Dw i'n dysgu Cymraeg ar y funud, felly mi dria i aros yn y Gymraeg gymaint ag y medra i — ond ella bydd raid i mi ofyn am help rŵan ac yn y man, os ydy hynny'n iawn.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| I'm learning Welsh at the moment | dw i'n dysgu Cymraeg ar y funud | D |  |
| so | felly | D |  |
| I'll try to stay in Welsh as much as I can | mi dria i aros yn y Gymraeg gymaint ag y medra i | D | kept distinct from 'as much as possible' → gymaint â phosib: two knowns, two forms, no fork |
| but I might need to ask for help | ond ella bydd raid i mi ofyn am help | I | same modal relocation as F01-S01 |
| now and then | rŵan ac yn y man | D |  |
| if that's alright | os ydy hynny'n iawn | D | converges with 'if that's okay' — reception, welcome |

*South:* *mi* → fe / bare · *ella* → falle · *medra i* → galla i · *rŵan* → nawr

**SC01-F02-S02** P — "Where are you learning, then? Are you doing the classes?"
> **Lle dach chi'n dysgu, 'ta? Dach chi'n gneud y dosbarthiadau?**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Where are you learning | Lle dach chi'n dysgu | D |  |
| then? | 'ta? | D | the in-that-case tag, per S1 — never wedyn, never felly here |
| Are you doing the classes? | Dach chi'n gneud y dosbarthiadau? | D | bare intonation question; Welsh needs no do-support |

*South:* *dach chi* → dych chi · *gneud* → gwneud · *'ta* → ?

**SC01-F02-S03** HW — "Through work, mostly - a little bit every day. I've been at it about a year now."
> **Drwy'r gwaith, gan fwya — rhyw chydig bob dydd. Dw i wrthi ers rhyw flwyddyn rŵan.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Through work, mostly | Drwy'r gwaith, gan fwya | D | HG08 verbatim |
| a little bit every day | rhyw chydig bob dydd | D |  |
| I've been at it about a year now | Dw i wrthi ers rhyw flwyddyn rŵan | I | the perfect-duration relocation: present + ers, and 'at it' becomes the conjugated wrthi |

*South:* *gan fwya* → gan fwyaf · *chydig* → tipyn / bach o · *rŵan* → nawr

**SC01-F02-S04** P — "A year? Well, you're doing marvellous. My grandson's learning in school, you know."
> **Blwyddyn? Wel, dach chi'n gneud yn ardderchog. Mae fy ŵyr i'n dysgu yn yr ysgol, wyddoch chi.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| A year? | Blwyddyn? | E | the English indefinite article erases — Welsh has none |
| Well | Wel | D |  |
| you're doing marvellous | dach chi'n gneud yn ardderchog | D | the adverbial yn is obligatory where English drops -ly |
| My grandson's learning in school | mae fy ŵyr i'n dysgu yn yr ysgol | D | the echo possessive fy…i |
| you know | wyddoch chi | D | the tag takes the irregular short gwybod, not a periphrasis |

*South:* *dach chi* → dych chi · *gneud* → gwneud

**SC01-F02-S05** HW — "That's lovely to hear. Right, shall we make a start? Just stop me if I lose you."
> **Mae hynna'n hyfryd i'w glywed. Reit — wnawn ni ddechra? Stopiwch fi os dw i'n eich colli chi.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| That's lovely to hear | Mae hynna'n hyfryd i'w glywed | I | the i'w + verbal-noun frame pre-poses the object English leaves stranded |
| Right | Reit | D |  |
| shall we make a start? | wnawn ni ddechra? | D | HG11 verbatim (R3) |
| Just stop me | Stopiwch fi | E | 'just' softener erased (R3) |
| if I lose you | os dw i'n eich colli chi | D | the eich…chi echo object |

*South:* *hynna* → hynny · *dechra* → dechre

**SC01-F02-S06** P — "Righto. I'll keep you right where I can."
> **Reit-o. Mi gadwa i chi'n iawn lle medra i.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Righto | Reit-o | D | the corpus's own ratifier; the borrow is register, not laziness (R6) |
| I'll keep you right | Mi gadwa i chi'n iawn | D | kept distinct from 'Right you are' → O'r gorau |
| where I can | lle medra i | D |  |

*South:* *mi* → fe / bare · *medra i* → galla i


**Flow 3**

**SC01-F03-S01** HW — "I'm learning Welsh at the moment, so I'll try to stay in Welsh, but if we get stuck I might ask you to speak English, if that's okay."
> **Dw i'n dysgu Cymraeg ar y funud, felly mi dria i aros yn y Gymraeg — ond os awn ni'n sownd, ella y gwna i ofyn i chi siarad Saesneg, os ydy hynny'n iawn.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| I'm learning Welsh at the moment | dw i'n dysgu Cymraeg ar y funud | D |  |
| so I'll try to stay in Welsh | felly mi dria i aros yn y Gymraeg | D |  |
| but if we get stuck | ond os awn ni'n sownd | D |  |
| I might ask you to speak English | ella y gwna i ofyn i chi siarad Saesneg | D | distinct from 'I might NEED to ask' → ella bydd raid i mi — one modal, one frame; no fork |
| if that's okay | os ydy hynny'n iawn | D |  |

*South:* *mi* → fe / bare · *ella* → falle

**SC01-F03-S02** P — "Actually, I'd rather stick to English today if that's alright, love. I'm not feeling up to it."
> **A deud y gwir, mae'n well gen i lynu at y Saesneg heddiw os ydy hynny'n iawn, del. Dw i ddim yn teimlo'n ddigon da i hynny.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Actually | A deud y gwir | I | English's sentence adverb relocates to a verbal-noun clause, 'and telling the truth' |
| I'd rather stick to English today | mae'n well gen i lynu at y Saesneg heddiw | I | preference relocates to the mae'n well gen i frame (HG57) — the N-form fork |
| if that's alright | os ydy hynny'n iawn | D |  |
| love | del | D | the patient's endearment to the young nurse; the corpus's own 'bach' register |
| I'm not feeling up to it | Dw i ddim yn teimlo'n ddigon da i hynny | I | English's 'up to it' capacity idiom relocates to a digon da sufficiency frame |

*South:* *deud* → dweud · *mae'n well gen i* → mae'n well gyda fi — AND A REORDER · *del* → neis / pert ?

*Southern line (re-ordered, not a token swap):* **A gweud y gwir, mae'n well gyda fi lynu at y Saesneg heddiw os yw hynny'n iawn, bach. Dw i ddim yn teimlo'n ddigon da i hynny.**

**SC01-F03-S03** HW — "Of course, no problem at all. English it is. Let's get you sorted."
> **Wrth gwrs, dim problem o gwbl. Saesneg amdani. Dewch i ni'ch sortio chi.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Of course | Wrth gwrs | D |  |
| no problem at all | dim problem o gwbl | D |  |
| English it is | Saesneg amdani | D | the X amdani ratifier (R3) — binds to scene 0's yield |
| Let's get you sorted | Dewch i ni'ch sortio chi | D | dewch i ni for 'let's' (R3); the wedi'ch sortio passive family (HG53) |

**SC01-F03-S04** P — "Thank you, love. No offence meant - I'm just tired today."
> **Diolch, del. Do'n i ddim yn trio'ch digio chi — jyst wedi blino ydw i heddiw.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Thank you | Diolch | D |  |
| love | del | D |  |
| No offence meant | Do'n i ddim yn trio'ch digio chi | I | English's agentless passive relocates to an agentive imperfect; digio is the walk's one offence-lexeme (HG04, HG07) |
| I'm just tired today | jyst wedi blino ydw i heddiw | I | identificatory fronting — Welsh puts the predicate first to carry English's 'just' |

*South:* *del* → neis / pert ?

**SC01-F03-S05** HW — "None taken at all. Right - first, I just need to take your blood pressure."
> **Dw i ddim wedi digio o gwbl. Reit — gynta, dw i jyst angen cymryd eich pwysau gwaed chi.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| None taken at all | Dw i ddim wedi digio o gwbl | I | HG07 verbatim — English's elliptical noun phrase becomes a full Welsh clause |
| Right | Reit | D |  |
| first | gynta | D |  |
| I just need to take your blood pressure | dw i jyst angen cymryd eich pwysau gwaed chi | D | 'just' outside the imperative keeps jyst (R3) |

*South:* *angen* → mae angen i fi · *gynta* → cynta'

**SC01-F03-S06** P — "Go on then. Do your worst."
> **Ewch amdani 'ta. Gnewch eich gwaetha.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Go on then | Ewch amdani 'ta | I | the amdani propulsion idiom; English's particle verb has no Welsh reflex |
| Do your worst | Gnewch eich gwaetha | D |  |

**Flow 4**

### Context 2.2 — Exploring the symptom


**Flow 1**

**SC15-F01-S01** HW — "When did it start? Is it there all the time, or does it come and go? Does anything make it better or worse?"
> **Pryd ddechreuodd o? Ydy o yna drwy'r amser, 'ta ydy o'n dod ac yn mynd? Oes 'na rywbeth yn ei neud o'n well neu'n waeth?**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| When did it start? | Pryd ddechreuodd o? | D | the plain preterite; no do-support |
| Is it there all the time, or does it come and go? | Ydy o yna drwy'r amser, 'ta ydy o'n dod ac yn mynd? | I | the alternative-question 'ta (§4), and Welsh must restate the whole verb in the second limb |
| Does anything make it better or worse? | Oes 'na rywbeth yn ei neud o'n well neu'n waeth? | I | English's 'anything' as subject relocates to an existential; the causative takes a resumptive ei |

*South:* *'ta* → neu ? · *ydy o* → yw e · *neud* → wneud

|---|---|---|---|
| About two weeks ago | Rhyw bythefnos yn ôl | D | pythefnos is one word — Welsh counts fortnights |
| It's there all the time, more or less | Mae o yna drwy'r amser, fwy neu lai | D |  |

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|

| how bad - one to ten? | pa mor ddrwg — un i ddeg? | D | pa mor is obligatory before a Welsh adjective |
| Has it stopped you doing anything? | Ydy o wedi'ch stopio chi rhag gneud rhywbeth? | I | the blocking preposition rhag, which English does not express |
| Work, sleep, the stairs? | Gwaith, cysgu, y grisiau? | D |  |

*South:* *mae o* → mae e · *gneud* → gwneud · *waetha* → waetha'

**SC16-F01-S01** HW — "Are you taking any medication at the moment? Anything over the counter? Any allergies that you know of?"

### Context 2.5 — Explaining what's going on


**Flow 1**

**SC18-F01-S01** HW — "So what I think is happening is a chest infection. That's why you've been coughing and feeling so tired. It's very common at this time of year."
> **Felly haint ar y frest sy'n digwydd, dw i'n meddwl. Dyna pam dach chi wedi bod yn pesychu ac yn teimlo mor flinedig. Mae o'n gyffredin iawn yr adeg yma o'r flwyddyn.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| So what I think is happening is a chest infection | Felly haint ar y frest sy'n digwydd, dw i'n meddwl | I | English's pseudo-cleft relocates to a sy'-focus with the belief-clause pushed to the end — Welsh cannot stack 'what I think is … is' |
| That's why you've been coughing and feeling so tired | Dyna pam dach chi wedi bod yn pesychu ac yn teimlo mor flinedig | D | the yn must be restated on the second participle |
| It's very common at this time of year | Mae o'n gyffredin iawn yr adeg yma o'r flwyddyn | D |  |

*South:* *dach chi* → dych chi · *mae o* → mae e

**SC18-F01-S02** P — "Ah, right. That makes sense, I suppose."
> **A, iawn. Mae hynna'n gneud synnwyr, am wn i.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ah, right | A, iawn | D |  |
| That makes sense | Mae hynna'n gneud synnwyr | D | the same synnwyr frame as HG05's 'doesn't make sense' |
| I suppose | am wn i | I | the fixed am wn i, which uses the irregular short gwybod — English's verb has no Welsh reflex here |

*South:* *gneud* → gwneud · *hynna* → hynny

**SC18-F01-S03** HW — "The good news is it should clear completely with treatment. Most people feel a lot better within three or four days."
> **Y newyddion da ydy y dylai o glirio'n llwyr efo triniaeth. Mae'r rhan fwya o bobl yn teimlo lot yn well o fewn tri neu bedwar diwrnod.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| The good news is it should clear completely with treatment | Y newyddion da ydy y dylai o glirio'n llwyr efo triniaeth | D | the identificatory ydy joining two clauses |
| Most people feel a lot better within three or four days | Mae'r rhan fwya o bobl yn teimlo lot yn well o fewn tri neu bedwar diwrnod | I | English's quantifier 'most' relocates to the noun phrase y rhan fwyaf o |

*South:* *efo* → gyda · *fwya* → fwyaf · *dylai o* → dylai e

**SC18-F01-S04** P — "Three or four days. I can cope with that."
> **Tri neu bedwar diwrnod. Mi fedra i ymdopi efo hynny.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Three or four days | Tri neu bedwar diwrnod | D | the numeral takes the singular in Welsh |
| I can cope with that | Mi fedra i ymdopi efo hynny | D | ymdopi for 'cope', kept apart from dod i ben for 'manage' |

*South:* *mi fedra i* → fe alla i · *efo* → gyda

**SC18-F01-S05** HW — "So here's the plan: a week of antibiotics, plenty of rest and fluids, and I want to hear from you if you're not clearly better by Friday."
> **Felly dyma'r cynllun: wythnos o wrthfiotigau, digon o orffwys a hylifau, a dw i isio clywed gynnoch chi os nad ydach chi'n amlwg well erbyn dydd Gwener.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| So here's the plan | Felly dyma'r cynllun | D |  |
| a week of antibiotics, plenty of rest and fluids | wythnos o wrthfiotigau, digon o orffwys a hylifau | D |  |
| and I want to hear from you if you're not clearly better by Friday | a dw i isio clywed gynnoch chi os nad ydach chi'n amlwg well erbyn dydd Gwener | I | English's 'from you' relocates to the conjugated gynnoch chi — hearing is a gan-relation in Welsh |

*South:* *isio* → eisiau / moyn · *gynnoch chi* → gyda chi · *ydach chi* → ydych chi

**SC18-F01-S06** P — "Makes sense. What do I do if it's the weekend, though?"
> **Gneud synnwyr. Ond be dw i'n neud os ydy hi'n benwythnos?**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Makes sense | Gneud synnwyr | E | English's subject erases in the elliptical reply; the bare verbal noun stands |
| What do I do if it's the weekend, though? | Ond be dw i'n neud os ydy hi'n benwythnos? | I | English's clause-final 'though' fronts as ond, because Welsh has no postposed concessive |

*South:* *gneud / neud* → gwneud / wneud · *ydy hi* → yw hi


**Flow 2**

**SC18-F02-S01** HW — "What I think is happening is a chest infection - that's the cough and the tiredness."
> **Haint ar y frest sy'n digwydd, dw i'n meddwl — dyna'r peswch a'r blinder.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| What I think is happening is a chest infection | Haint ar y frest sy'n digwydd, dw i'n meddwl | I |  |
| that's the cough and the tiredness | dyna'r peswch a'r blinder | D |  |

**SC18-F02-S02** P — "Sorry, could you explain that again? What does that mean, exactly?"
> **Sori, fedrwch chi esbonio hynny eto? Be'n union mae hynny'n ei olygu?**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sorry | Sori | D |  |
| could you explain that again? | fedrwch chi esbonio hynny eto? | D | the conditional-as-request lands on the plain fedrwch chi — Welsh does not stack politeness here |
| What does that mean, exactly? | Be'n union mae hynny'n ei olygu? | I | 'exactly' fronts with the interrogative; the resumptive ei is obligatory |

*South:* *fedrwch chi* → allwch chi

**SC18-F02-S03** HW — "Of course. There's an infection in the tubes of your lungs - a bug, basically. That's what's causing the cough and the tiredness. The tablets will kill the bug."
> **Wrth gwrs. Mae 'na haint yn nhiwbiau'ch ysgyfaint chi — chwilen, yn y bôn. Dyna sy'n achosi'r peswch a'r blinder. Mi wneith y tabledi ladd y chwilen.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Of course | Wrth gwrs | D |  |
| There's an infection in the tubes of your lungs | Mae 'na haint yn nhiwbiau'ch ysgyfaint chi | D | the mae 'na existential; the nasal mutation after yn absorbed |
| a bug, basically | chwilen, yn y bôn | E | the indefinite article erases; chwilen is the plain-speech bug, matching the doctor's own register shift |
| That's what's causing the cough and the tiredness | Dyna sy'n achosi'r peswch a'r blinder | I | the sy'-focus does English's cleft without any dummy |
| The tablets will kill the bug | Mi wneith y tabledi ladd y chwilen | D |  |

*South:* *mi wneith* → fe wnaiff

**SC18-F02-S04** P — "A bug in the tubes. Right. Now I understand you."
> **Chwilen yn y tiwbiau. Iawn. Rŵan dw i'n eich dallt chi.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| A bug in the tubes | Chwilen yn y tiwbiau | E | the indefinite article erases |
| Right. Now I understand you | Iawn. Rŵan dw i'n eich dallt chi | D | RE-CUT (fork F2): 'Right' alone opens a move and takes Reit; here it receipts one, so the unit is the whole receipting move and the fork dies inside it |

*South:* *rŵan* → nawr · *dallt* → deall

**SC18-F02-S05** HW — "That's it exactly. Does the rest make sense? What questions have you got for me?"
> **Dyna fo'n union. Ydy'r gweddill yn gneud synnwyr? Pa gwestiynau sy gynnoch chi i mi?**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| That's it exactly | Dyna fo'n union | D |  |
| Does the rest make sense? | Ydy'r gweddill yn gneud synnwyr? | D |  |
| What questions have you got for me? | Pa gwestiynau sy gynnoch chi i mi? | I | possession relocates to the sy' gan relative |

*South:* *dyna fo* → dyna fe · *gneud* → gwneud · *sy gynnoch chi* → sy gyda chi

**SC18-F02-S06** P — "No questions. You've explained it lovely."
> **Dim cwestiynau. Dach chi wedi'i esbonio fo'n hyfryd.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| No questions | Dim cwestiynau | D |  |
| You've explained it lovely | Dach chi wedi'i esbonio fo'n hyfryd | D | the English dialect's bare adjective takes the Welsh adverbial yn, which the standard requires |

*South:* *dach chi* → dych chi · *fo* → fe


**Flow 3**

**SC18-F03-S01** HW — "What I think is happening is a chest infection - common at this time of year."
> **Haint ar y frest sy'n digwydd, dw i'n meddwl — cyffredin yr adeg yma o'r flwyddyn.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| What I think is happening is a chest infection | Haint ar y frest sy'n digwydd, dw i'n meddwl | I |  |
| common at this time of year | cyffredin yr adeg yma o'r flwyddyn | D |  |

**SC18-F03-S02** P — "So it's nothing to do with my heart, then? That's what I was really worried about."
> **Felly does 'na ddim byd i'w neud efo fy nghalon i, 'ta? Dyna be ro'n i wir yn poeni amdano fo.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| So it's nothing to do with my heart, then? | Felly does 'na ddim byd i'w neud efo fy nghalon i, 'ta? | I | English's negative subject relocates to a negative existential; 'so' → felly and 'then' → 'ta stand side by side, each in its own role (S1) |
| That's what I was really worried about | Dyna be ro'n i wir yn poeni amdano fo | D | 'worry about' conjugates as amdano fo |

*South:* *neud* → wneud · *efo* → gyda · *'ta* → ? · *amdano fo* → amdano fe

**SC18-F03-S03** HW — "Your heart is fine - I've listened to it, and it's strong and steady. This is the lungs. If I were worried, I would tell you straight."
> **Mae'ch calon chi'n iawn — dw i wedi gwrando arni hi, ac mae hi'n gry ac yn gyson. Yr ysgyfaint ydy hyn. Taswn i'n poeni, mi faswn i'n deud wrthach chi'n blaen.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Your heart is fine | Mae'ch calon chi'n iawn | D |  |
| I've listened to it | dw i wedi gwrando arni hi | D | gwrando governs ar; calon is feminine, so arni hi |
| and it's strong and steady | ac mae hi'n gry ac yn gyson | D | the yn must be restated on the second adjective |
| This is the lungs | Yr ysgyfaint ydy hyn | I | identificatory fronting reverses English's order |
| If I were worried, I would tell you straight | Taswn i'n poeni, mi faswn i'n deud wrthach chi'n blaen | D | the tas- conditional, which English marks with a past subjunctive |

*South:* *cry* → cryf · *taswn i / mi faswn i* → tawn i / fe fyddwn i · *deud / wrthach chi* → gweud / wrthoch chi

**SC18-F03-S04** P — "You'd tell me straight. Good. That's all I ask, doctor."
> **Mi fasach chi'n deud wrtha i'n blaen. Da iawn. Dyna'r cwbl dw i'n ei ofyn, doctor.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| You'd tell me straight | Mi fasach chi'n deud wrtha i'n blaen | D |  |
| Good | Da iawn | D | consolidated onto the walk’s one approval-word — see §3, fork F1 |
| That's all I ask, doctor | Dyna'r cwbl dw i'n ei ofyn, doctor | D | the object relative with a resumptive ei |

*South:* *mi fasach chi* → fe fyddech chi · *deud / wrtha i* → gweud / wrtho i

**SC18-F03-S05** HW — "Always. So - a week of antibiotics, rest, and fluids, and ring me if you're not better by Friday."
> **Bob amser. Felly — wythnos o wrthfiotigau, gorffwys a hylifau, a ffoniwch fi os nad ydach chi'n well erbyn dydd Gwener.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Always | Bob amser | D |  |
| So - a week of antibiotics, rest, and fluids | Felly — wythnos o wrthfiotigau, gorffwys a hylifau | D |  |
| and ring me if you're not better by Friday | a ffoniwch fi os nad ydach chi'n well erbyn dydd Gwener | D | ffonio for telephoning, against canu for a bell |

*South:* *ydach chi* → ydych chi

**SC18-F03-S06** P — "Right. And what if it's the weekend?"
> **Iawn. A be os ydy hi'n benwythnos?**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Right. And what if it's the weekend? | Iawn. A be os ydy hi'n benwythnos? | D | RE-CUT (fork F2) — as SC18-F02-S04 |

### Context 2.6 — Ordering tests


**Flow 1**

**SC19-F01-S01** HW — "I'd like to send you for a blood test, just to rule a couple of things out. The nurse will do it today, and we should have results by Friday."
> **Mi faswn i'n licio'ch anfon chi am brawf gwaed, jyst i ddiystyru cwpwl o betha. Mi wneith y nyrs o heddiw, ac mi ddylen ni gael canlyniadau erbyn dydd Gwener.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| I'd like to send you for a blood test | Mi faswn i'n licio'ch anfon chi am brawf gwaed | D |  |
| just to rule a couple of things out | jyst i ddiystyru cwpwl o betha | E | English's split particle 'rule out' has nothing to split in Welsh — diystyru carries it |
| The nurse will do it today | Mi wneith y nyrs o heddiw | D |  |
| and we should have results by Friday | ac mi ddylen ni gael canlyniadau erbyn dydd Gwener | D |  |

*South:* *mi faswn i* → fe fyddwn i · *licio* → hoffi · *petha* → pethe · *mi wneith* → fe wnaiff

**SC19-F01-S02** P — "Fine by me. Whatever you think best."
> **Mae hynny'n iawn gen i. Beth bynnag dach chi'n feddwl sy ora.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Fine by me | Mae hynny'n iawn gen i | I | English's verbless fragment needs a Welsh clause; 'by me' becomes the conjugated gen i |
| Whatever you think best | Beth bynnag dach chi'n feddwl sy ora | I | the bynnag free-relative, with a sy' relative under it that English can leave out |

*South:* *gen i* → gyda fi · *dach chi* → dych chi · *ora* → orau

**SC19-F01-S03** HW — "Good. It's a simple one - no fasting needed, and it takes two minutes."
> **Da iawn. Un syml ydy o — dim angen ymprydio, ac mae o'n cymryd dau funud.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Good | Da iawn | D |  |
| It's a simple one | Un syml ydy o | I | identificatory fronting; the indefinite article erases |
| no fasting needed | dim angen ymprydio | D | the bare negative angen frame (R3), as at 'no need to apologise' |
| and it takes two minutes | ac mae o'n cymryd dau funud | D |  |

*South:* *mae o* → mae e

**SC19-F01-S04** P — "Look and know rather than guess. I like that."
> **Edrych a gwybod yn hytrach na dyfalu. Dw i'n licio hynna.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Look and know rather than guess | Edrych a gwybod yn hytrach na dyfalu | D | gwybod for factual knowing (S5) |
| I like that | Dw i'n licio hynna | D |  |

*South:* *licio* → hoffi · *hynna* → hynny

**SC19-F01-S05** HW — "Exactly right. Keep taking the tablets in the meantime - don't wait for the results."
> **Yn union. Daliwch i gymryd y tabledi yn y cyfamser — peidiwch ag aros am y canlyniadau.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Exactly right | Yn union | E | English's 'right' erases — yn union alone is the Welsh ratifier |
| Keep taking the tablets in the meantime | Daliwch i gymryd y tabledi yn y cyfamser | D | dal i + verbal noun for 'keep -ing' |
| don't wait for the results | peidiwch ag aros am y canlyniadau | D | peidiwch ag before a vowel; aros governs am |

**SC19-F01-S06** P — "Righto. Tablets regardless. Thank you, doctor."
> **Reit-o. Tabledi beth bynnag. Diolch, doctor.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Righto | Reit-o | D |  |
| Tablets regardless | Tabledi beth bynnag | D |  |
| Thank you, doctor | Diolch, doctor | D |  |


**Flow 2**

**SC19-F02-S01** HW — "I'd like to send you for a blood test, just to rule a couple of things out."
> **Mi faswn i'n licio'ch anfon chi am brawf gwaed, jyst i ddiystyru cwpwl o betha.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| I'd like to send you for a blood test | Mi faswn i'n licio'ch anfon chi am brawf gwaed | D |  |
| just to rule a couple of things out | jyst i ddiystyru cwpwl o betha | E |  |

*South:* *petha* → pethe · *licio* → hoffi

**SC19-F02-S02** P — "What are you testing for, exactly? Should I be worried?"
> **Am be'n union dach chi'n profi? Ddylwn i boeni?**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| What are you testing for, exactly? | Am be'n union dach chi'n profi? | I | Welsh fronts the preposition with the interrogative — English strands it |
| Should I be worried? | Ddylwn i boeni? | I | Welsh has no be-worried passive here — the verb poeni carries it |

*South:* *dach chi* → dych chi

**SC19-F02-S03** HW — "Infection and anaemia, mainly. The tiredness could just be the infection, but I'd rather look and know than guess."
> **Haint ac anemia, gan fwya. Mi allai'r blinder fod yn haint yn unig, ond mae'n well gen i edrych a gwybod na dyfalu.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Infection and anaemia, mainly | Haint ac anemia, gan fwya | D |  |
| The tiredness could just be the infection | Mi allai'r blinder fod yn haint yn unig | I | 'just' here is restrictive and lands clause-finally as yn unig, not as jyst and not erased |
| but I'd rather look and know than guess | ond mae'n well gen i edrych a gwybod na dyfalu | I | preference relocates to mae'n well gen i — the N-form fork |

*South:* *gan fwya* → gan fwyaf · *mae'n well gen i* → mae'n well gyda fi — AND A REORDER · *mi allai* → fe allai

*Southern line (re-ordered, not a token swap):* **Haint ac anemia, gan fwyaf. Fe allai'r blinder fod yn haint yn unig, ond mae'n well gyda fi edrych a gwybod na dyfalu.**

**SC19-F02-S04** P — "That's fair enough. Do I do it now?"
> **Mae hynny'n ddigon teg. Ydw i'n ei neud o rŵan?**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| That's fair enough | Mae hynny'n ddigon teg | I | Welsh puts digon before the adjective, where English puts 'enough' after |
| Do I do it now? | Ydw i'n ei neud o rŵan? | D | no do-support; the resumptive ei |

*South:* *neud* → wneud · *rŵan* → nawr

**SC19-F02-S05** HW — "The nurse can do it today - and we'll text you if it's normal, or ring you if anything needs a chat."
> **Mi fedar y nyrs ei neud o heddiw — ac mi wnawn ni decstio os ydy o'n normal, neu ffonio os oes 'na rywbeth angen sgwrs.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| The nurse can do it today | Mi fedar y nyrs ei neud o heddiw | D |  |
| and we'll text you if it's normal | ac mi wnawn ni decstio os ydy o'n normal | E | English's object 'you' erases — tecstio is intransitive in this register |
| or ring you if anything needs a chat | neu ffonio os oes 'na rywbeth angen sgwrs | E | 'anything' → rhywbeth inside the existential (S9) |

*South:* *mi fedar* → fe all · *neud* → wneud · *ydy o* → yw e

> **Tecst os ydy o'n normal, galwad os nad ydy o. Dallt.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| A text if it's normal, a call if it's not | Tecst os ydy o'n normal, galwad os nad ydy o | I | English's gapped 'if it's not' cannot gap in Welsh — the verb must return |
| Understood | Dallt | I |  |

*South:* *ydy o* → yw e · *dallt* → deall

**SC19-F03-S01** HW — "I'd like the nurse to take some blood today, and we'll have results by Friday."
> **Mi faswn i'n licio i'r nyrs gymryd gwaed heddiw, ac mi gawn ni ganlyniadau erbyn dydd Gwener.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| I'd like the nurse to take some blood today | Mi faswn i'n licio i'r nyrs gymryd gwaed heddiw | I | English's ECM relocates to licio i + subject + verbal noun; 'some' erases |
| and we'll have results by Friday | ac mi gawn ni ganlyniadau erbyn dydd Gwener | D |  |

|---|---|---|---|
| Tomorrow's fine | Mae fory'n iawn | D |  |
| book at the desk on your way out | archebwch wrth y ddesg ar eich ffordd allan | D |  |

|---|---|---|---|

**SC19-F03-S05** HW — "Book at the desk to be safe. And keep taking the tablets in the meantime - don't wait for results."
> **Archebwch wrth y ddesg i fod yn saff. A daliwch i gymryd y tabledi yn y cyfamser — peidiwch ag aros am ganlyniadau.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Book at the desk to be safe | Archebwch wrth y ddesg i fod yn saff | D |  |

| don't wait for results | peidiwch ag aros am ganlyniadau | D | distinct from 'don't wait for THE results' → …am y canlyniadau |

*South:* *saff* → ?

**SC19-F03-S06** P — "Text good, call thorough, tablets regardless. You've a way of making it simple."
> **Tecst yn dda, galwad yn drylwyr, tabledi beth bynnag. Mae gynnoch chi ffordd o'i neud o'n syml.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Text good, call thorough, tablets regardless | Tecst yn dda, galwad yn drylwyr, tabledi beth bynnag | I | English's telegraphic apposition needs the Welsh predicative yn on each adjective |
| You've a way of making it simple | Mae gynnoch chi ffordd o'i neud o'n syml | I | possession relocates to the existential with conjugated gan |

*South:* *mae gynnoch chi* → mae … gyda chi — AND A REORDER · *neud* → wneud

*Southern line (re-ordered, not a token swap):* **Tecst yn dda, galwad yn drylwyr, tabledi beth bynnag. Mae ffordd gyda chi o'i wneud e'n syml.**

|---|---|---|---|
| None, doctor | Dim un, doctor | D |  |
| Thank you for being so thorough | Diolch am fod mor drylwyr | D | the am + verbal noun causal |


### Context 2.9 — Giving results


**Flow 1**

**SC22-F01-S01** HW — "Your results have come back and I'm pleased to say everything looks normal. Your blood sugar was a little on the high side, so let's keep an eye on that."
> **Mae'ch canlyniadau chi wedi dod yn ôl, ac mae'n dda gen i ddeud bod bob dim yn edrych yn normal. Roedd eich siwgr gwaed chi braidd yn uchel, felly dewch i ni gadw golwg ar hynny.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Your results have come back | Mae'ch canlyniadau chi wedi dod yn ôl | D |  |
| and I'm pleased to say everything looks normal | ac mae'n dda gen i ddeud bod bob dim yn edrych yn normal | I | pleasure relocates to mae'n dda gen i — the N-form fork; the bod that-clause under it |
| Your blood sugar was a little on the high side | Roedd eich siwgr gwaed chi braidd yn uchel | E | English's 'on the high side' hedge erases into braidd yn |
| so let's keep an eye on that | felly dewch i ni gadw golwg ar hynny | E | English's 'an eye' erases into the fixed cadw golwg |

*South:* *mae'n dda gen i* → mae'n dda gyda fi — AND A REORDER · *deud* → gweud · *bob dim* → popeth

*Southern line (re-ordered, not a token swap):* **Mae'ch canlyniadau chi wedi dod yn ôl, ac mae'n dda gyda fi weud bod popeth yn edrych yn normal. Roedd eich siwgr gwaed chi braidd yn uchel, felly dewch i ni gadw golwg ar hynny.**

**SC22-F01-S02** P — "Oh, thank goodness for that. What a relief."
> **O, diolch byth am hynny. Am ryddhad.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Oh, thank goodness for that | O, diolch byth am hynny | I | the fixed diolch byth; English's 'goodness' has no Welsh reflex |
| What a relief | Am ryddhad | I | Welsh's exclamative takes am, not the interrogative word English uses |

**SC22-F01-S03** HW — "It is good news. And the sugar is only just over the line - think of it as a nudge, well before any diagnosis."
> **Mae o'n newyddion da, ydy. Ac dim ond prin dros y llinell mae'r siwgr — meddyliwch amdano fo fel pwniad, ymhell cyn unrhyw ddiagnosis.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| It is good news | Mae o'n newyddion da, ydy | I | English marks the emphasis with a stressed auxiliary; Welsh appends the verb-echo ydy |
| And the sugar is only just over the line | Ac dim ond prin dros y llinell mae'r siwgr | I | the whole phrase fronts for focus, which English does with stress alone; 'only just' takes dim ond prin, not the erasing softener |
| think of it as a nudge, well before any diagnosis | meddyliwch amdano fo fel pwniad, ymhell cyn unrhyw ddiagnosis | D | meddwl governs am, conjugated as amdano fo |

*South:* *mae o* → mae e · *amdano fo* → amdano fe

**SC22-F01-S04** P — "At the doorstep. So I can still turn it round, can I?"

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| At the doorstep | Ar y trothwy | D |  |
| So I can still turn it round, can I? | Felly mi fedra i ddal i'w droi o rownd, medra? | I | the verb-echo tag medra — Welsh tags with the verb, and here the polarity stays positive |

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|

**Flow 1**

|---|---|---|---|

| I'm glad you've told me | dw i'n falch eich bod chi wedi deud wrtha i | D | ⚠ line — the eich bod chi that-clause; 'tell' governs wrth |
| Show me exactly where | Dangoswch i mi'n union lle | D | ⚠ line — dangos governs i |
| and I'll examine you properly now rather than guessing | ac mi wna i'ch archwilio chi'n iawn rŵan yn hytrach na dyfalu | D | ⚠ line |

*South:* *hynna* → hynny · *deud / wrtha i* → gweud / wrtho i · *rŵan* → nawr

**SC23-F03-S04** P ⚠ — "Just here, doctor - and it woke me at four. It's different from before. Sharper."
> **Yn union fan'ma, doctor — ac mi wnaeth hi fy neffro i am bedwar. Mae hi'n wahanol i o'r blaen. Yn finiocach.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Just here, doctor | Yn union fan'ma, doctor | I | ⚠ line — precise 'just', so yn union, not the erasing softener |
| and it woke me at four | ac mi wnaeth hi fy neffro i am bedwar | D | ⚠ line — feminine hi for the pain (R8) |
| It's different from before | Mae hi'n wahanol i o'r blaen | I | ⚠ line — Welsh's 'different' governs i, not the from-preposition English uses |
| Sharper | Yn finiocach | D | ⚠ line — the synthetic comparative with its obligatory predicative yn |

*South:* *fan'ma* → fan hyn · *mi wnaeth hi* → fe wnaeth hi

**SC23-F03-S05** HW ⚠ — "Here's what happens next: I'll examine you now, we'll take some bloods, and I'll come back to you myself before lunch with a plan. You won't be left wondering."
> **Dyma be sy'n digwydd nesa: mi wna i'ch archwilio chi rŵan, mi gymrwn ni waed, ac mi ddo i'n ôl atoch chi fy hun cyn cinio efo cynllun. Chewch chi mo'ch gadael yn pendroni.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Here's what happens next | Dyma be sy'n digwydd nesa | D | ⚠ line — the sy'-focus free relative |
| I'll examine you now | mi wna i'ch archwilio chi rŵan | D | ⚠ line |
| we'll take some bloods | mi gymrwn ni waed | E | ⚠ line — English's clinical plural 'bloods' erases; gwaed is a mass noun |
| and I'll come back to you myself before lunch with a plan | ac mi ddo i'n ôl atoch chi fy hun cyn cinio efo cynllun | D | ⚠ line — the promise the whole scene turns on |
| You won't be left wondering | Chewch chi mo'ch gadael yn pendroni | I | ⚠ line — Welsh's cael-passive in its negative, with the mo contraction |

*South:* *nesa* → nesa' · *rŵan* → nawr · *efo* → gyda · *mi ddo i* → fe ddo i

**SC23-F03-S06** P ⚠ — "Before lunch. Thank you, doctor. That's all I wanted - to know what's happening."
> **Cyn cinio. Diolch, doctor. Dyna'r cwbl ro'n i isio — gwybod be sy'n digwydd.**

| chunk | Welsh | class | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Before lunch | Cyn cinio | D | ⚠ line |
| Thank you, doctor | Diolch, doctor | D |  |
| That's all I wanted | Dyna'r cwbl ro'n i isio | D |  |
| to know what's happening | gwybod be sy'n digwydd | D | ⚠ line — gwybod for factual knowing (S5); the sy'-focus free relative |

*South:* *isio* → eisiau / moyn

---

## 2. The four classes, in numbers

Counted mechanically off §1's tables — **1,278 classified chunk rows across 438 turns**, an average
of 2.9 cut units per turn, which is what conversation granularity looks like when the unit is the
unit of intention rather than the word.

| class | rows | share | what it means here |
|---|---|---|---|
| **deterministic** | 785 | 61% | chunk → one Welsh form, structure survives; mutations, conjugated prepositions, echo pronouns and gender agreement all absorbed inside the chunk |
| **inversion** | 389 | 30% | the frame relocates: possession → *gen i*, perfect-duration → present + *ers*, preference and regret → *mae'n well / ddrwg gen i*, clefts, existentials, the *â*-carrying and *â*-propulsion frames, argument swaps |
| **erasure** | 101 | 8% | an English distinction Welsh does not carry: the indefinite article, "just" as softener, "ever", "all", reflexives, plural *lights* / *glasses* / *bloods*, dummy *do*, emphatic *do*, particle *up / off / on* |
| **split** | 3 | 0.2% | English wanted to fork; every one re-cut or pinned in §3, none blocked |

**Against the 57-seed cut's 142 / 45 / 10 / 8 (69% / 22% / 5% / 4%):** deterministic falls eight
points and inversion rises eight. That is not a worse result, and it is not noise — it is what
happens when you leave a curated seed set and enter **real conversation**. The 57 seeds were chosen
to teach; these 438 turns were written to be *said*, and unchosen speech is thick with exactly the
things Welsh relocates rather than translates: possession ("I've got it now", "you've a knack with
pillows", "the doctor will have the results"), stranded English particles ("hang on", "bring out
in", "sign off", "set off", "pick up"), pro-verbs ("lots of people do", "I will do", "that helps,
that does"), and the whole English feelings-as-adjectives family that Welsh puts on prepositions
instead (*mae gen i ofn*, *wrth ei bodd*, *dw i ar lwgu*, *mae'n ddrwg gen i*).

**The headline holds anyway: at conversation granularity, 91% of this walk's Welsh is either
deterministic or a clean whole-frame relocation, and only three chunks in 1,278 forked at all.**
The 0.2% split rate — against 4% in the seed set — is the granularity ruling doing its work
visibly: with bigger units, the would-be forks die inside the chunk before they reach production.

---

## 3. The splits, the forks, and the convergences

### 3.1 The three splits

All three are the same fork — Welsh's numerals and "both" carry gender where English's do not — and
all three are resolved by reading the cast, not by rule.

| turn | English | Welsh | why |
|---|---|---|---|
| SC01-F06-S05 | "with you both here" | *efo chi'ch **dwy** yma* | the pair is the patient and her daughter — feminine |
| SC12-F03-S05 | "Put the sheet somewhere you'll both see it" | *…y gwelwch chi'ch **dau** hi* | the pair is the patient and her husband — mixed, so Welsh takes masculine |
| SC13-F04-S05 | "all three of us" | *ni'n **tair*** | doctor, patient and her daughter; the corpus does not sex the doctor, so the two women decide it |

The 57-seed cut flagged this as hazard S3 and pinned it feminine course-wide. **This corpus proves
the hazard was real**: the same English chunk, "you'll both see it", takes *dwy* in one context and
*dau* in another, because the world behind it is different. They are different knowns, not a
re-mapping of one. Nothing here contradicts the seed set — it extends it.

### 3.2 The four forks the sweep caught, and the re-cut that killed each

The whole corpus was swept for same-normalised-known → two-Welsh-forms. Four came back. Per the
ruling — *where a cut diverges, re-cut the unit rather than blocking a word* — every one was
resolved by moving the cut, and none by refusing a word.

- **F1 — "Good"** took *Da iawn* nine times and a bare *Da* once. Not a real sense split, just a
  slip: **consolidated on *Da iawn***. One intention, one form.
- **F2 — "Right"** genuinely does two jobs in this corpus: it *opens* a move ("Right, shall we make
  a start?") and it *receipts* one ("A bug in the tubes. Right."). Welsh will not take one word for
  both — the opener is *Reit*, the receipt is *Iawn*. **Re-cut**: the two receipting occurrences
  fold into the whole receipting move ("Right. Now I understand you" → *Iawn. Rŵan dw i'n eich
  dallt chi*), so the bare unit "Right" only ever means *Reit* and the fork dies inside the larger
  chunk. This is the single best exhibit in the walk for why chunk size is the instrument.
- **F3 — "There"** took *Dyna ni* once and *Dyna fo* once, for the same presentative gesture:
  **consolidated on *Dyna ni***. It now converges with "There we are", which is reception and
  welcome.
- **F4 — "Never double up"** is the same English string in the doctor's mouth and the patient's,
  one sentence apart — but they are different moves, and **Welsh cannot echo an imperative as an
  imperative** the way English can. **Re-cut**: the patient's unit is the whole ratifying move
  ("Never double up. Right." → *Peidio byth â dyblu. Reit.*), which no other turn carries, so the
  fork dies inside it.

**The sweep was re-run after the re-cuts and came back clean: zero same-known → two-targets across
all 438 turns and all 1,278 chunk rows.**

### 3.3 The convergences — welcome, and listed

ZUT is one-directional. Several English knowns landing on one Welsh form is *reception*, and it is
a teaching gift: the learner meets one form and it does more work than the English did. Twenty-eight
of them, and the interesting ones are the register-flatteners — English's *Ta* and *Thank you*
both land on *Diolch*, its *Grand* and *Champion* both on *Campus*.

| Welsh form | English knowns landing on it |
|---|---|
| os ydy hynny'n iawn | if that's okay · if that's alright |
| mae hynny'n iawn | that's fine · That's alright |
| Diolch | Diolch · Thank you · thank you · Ta |
| mae hynna'n glên iawn | that's very kind · That's kind of you |
| os dw i'n deud rhywbeth sydd ddim yn glir | if anything I say isn't clear · If I say anything that isn't clear |
| Mi wna i | I will · Will do |
| 'ta? | then? · then · in that case |
| Wel | Well · Well! · Well? |
| A deud y gwir | Actually · to be honest · actually · To be honest |
| mi esbonia i'n gliriach | I'll explain myself more clearly · I'll explain more clearly |
| ylwch | see · See? |
| Siân dw i | my name's Siân · I'm Siân |
| Fedrwch chi ddeud eich dyddiad geni wrtha i? | Can you tell me your date of birth? · can you tell me your date of birth |
| Dyna ni | There · There we are |
| Cyn i ni ddechra | Before we start · Before we begin |
| Campus | Grand · Champion |
| a deg ydy'r gwaetha fedrwch chi ddychmygu? | with ten the worst you can imagine? · ten being the worst you can imagine? |
| Dim i ddeg | Nought to ten · nought to ten? |
| mae'r llall ar ôl cinio | the other one's after lunch · the other's after lunch |
| Dallt | Understood · Got it |
| Pwyswch ar hwnna i mi | Just press on that for me · press on that for me |
| mae 'na lot o bobl yn gneud | lots of people do · A lot of people do |
| Arhoswch funud | Hang on · Hold on |
| Mae hynny'n iawn gen i | That's fine by me · Fine by me |
| Medda'r derbynnydd, roeddech chi isio fy ngweld i | The receptionist said you wanted to see me · The receptionist said you wanted to see me? |
| Diolch, doctor | Thank you, doctor · Ta, doctor |
| Cytuno? | Deal? · Deal |
| ond roedd eich siwgr gwaed chi braidd yn uchel | but your blood sugar was a little on the high side · but your blood sugar was a little high |
---

## 4. Where the curriculum relocates for Welsh

What the Welsh side *requires* that the English side never shows. At conversation granularity the
learner meets all of it **inside worn chunks, never as grammar** — but this is the list of what the
strand is actually leaning on, most load-bearing first, with the contexts that carry it.

1. **Conjugated prepositions — the single heaviest dependency, in nearly every turn.**
   *wrtha i, wrthach chi, wrthan ni, wrtho fo, arna i, arnoch chi, arni hi, amdano fo, amdani,
   amdanoch, amdanyn nhw, ato, atoch chi, iddo fo, iddi hi, iddyn nhw, ohonan ni, ohonoch chi,
   ohonyn nhw, gen i, gynnoch chi, gynnon ni, drwyddo fo, drwyddi, ynoch chi, ynddi*. English
   strands its prepositions; Welsh cannot, so the learner meets a conjugated form every time a
   sentence has an indirect object at all.
2. **The *gen i* possessive family, and everything English predicates that Welsh possesses.**
   *mae gen i* (have), *mae'n well gen i* (prefer), *mae'n ddrwg gen i* (be sorry), *mae'n dda gen
   i* (be pleased), *mae gen i ofn* (be frightened), *mae … gan y doctor* (the doctor will have),
   *wrth ei bodd* (be delighted), *dw i ar lwgu* (be starving). This is the largest single source
   of inversions in the walk and the only place the north/south fork changes a sentence's shape.
3. **Verb-echo answers and tags, in place of English's pronoun-plus-auxiliary.** *Ydy · Oes ·
   Cewch · Naddo · Ia · Medra · Gnewch · Does · Wnewch chi*. English answers "You can" with a
   pronoun; Welsh answers with the verb, and it must be the right verb. Contexts 1.2, 1.5, 2.2,
   2.5, 2.9 all turn on one.
4. **The *-wch* imperative — the walk's whole production register**, and *peidiwch â* for its
   negative. *stopiwch, deudwch, gofynnwch, rhowch, pwyswch, cymrwch, gafaelwch, steddwch, canwch,
   triwch, ylwch, dewch, gadwch, daliwch, torrwch, dangoswch, anadlwch, gwrandwch, archebwch,
   gorffennwch, gaddewch, edrychwch, gnewch, arhoswch, taniwch*.
5. **Present + *ers* for have-been-Xing** — the perfect-duration relocation. 1.0, 2.1, 2.10.
6. **Clefts and focus.** The *sy'*-focus (*hi sy'n dod i mewn*, *y bobl sy'n poeni*, *Y boen
   ddaeth gynta*), the *mai*-cleft (*mai chi ydy'r person iawn*), identificatory fronting (*Siân dw
   i*, *dim Saesneg ydy fy iaith gynta i*, *Pedwar deg wyth oedd o*), and the appositive *sef*.
   Welsh's default answer to English's stress-for-emphasis.
7. **Clause-frames**: *cyn i / tra / fel bod / nes / rhag ofn / os / pan / hyd yn oed os*, and the
   *bod* family (*eich bod chi, fy mod i, ei fod o*) for that-clauses.
8. **The *wedi*- and *cael*-passives.** *wedi'i nodi, wedi'ch sortio, wedi'ch cyflogi, wedi'i
   sgwennu, cael y sgan wedi'i neud, chewch chi mo'ch gadael*. English's be-passive and get-passive
   land on two different Welsh machines.
9. **The pre-posed object before a verbal noun** — *i'w gofio, i'w fwyta, i'w gymryd, i'w neud,
   eich cyfarfod chi, ei wybod*. English leaves the object stranded after the infinitive; Welsh
   must move it in front as a possessive.
10. **The *â*-family.** Carrying (*dod â, mynd â, dod â chi i mewn*), propulsion (*ffwrdd â ni /
    â chi*), comparison (*mor dda â hyn*), and the negative imperative's *â / ag*.
11. **Small preposition semantics English does not distinguish**: remedial *at* (*rhywbeth at y
    boen*), motion-toward *at* (*at y drws*, *atoch chi*), blocking *rhag* (*eich stopio chi rhag
    symud*), summons *ar* (*galw ar rywun*), governing *wrth* (*deud wrth*, *da wrtha i*,
    *pregethu wrth*), and illness's *ar* (*roedd o ar fy mam i*).
12. **Gender**, on nouns, numerals and adjectives — *y ddwy wen*, *tri pheth*, *ni'n tair*, *chi'ch
    dau*, *tair blynedd*, *dwy botel fach*, *y boen … mae hi*, and the ambient feminine *hi*.
13. **Negative concord.** *Ddeudodd neb ddim byd*, *Does neb erioed wedi…*, *Chafodd hi erioed…*.
    English allows one negative per clause; Welsh stacks them and requires it.
14. **Bare embedded yes/no questions** — *gawn ni weld fedrwch chi ei orffen o* — where English
    needs "if" or "whether" and Welsh needs nothing.
15. **The alternative-question *'ta*** — *y cyw iâr 'ta'r cawl*, *pan dw i'n gwasgu i lawr 'ta pan
    dw i'n gollwng*. Load-bearing in 2.4, where the rebound-tenderness question depends on the two
    limbs being unmistakable.
16. **Numbers under pressure** — decimal readings, clock times (*hanner awr wedi dau*), *pythefnos*
    for a fortnight, feminine counting with *blynedd*, and years as whole numbers.
17. **The colloquial reductions that ARE the northern register** — *dechra, gynta, ola, nesa, hyna,
    dwytha, gora, leia, fwya, hynna, gola, petha, cry, cyfri, difri, peryg, sgwyddau, gan fwya,
    stafell molchi*. Not sloppiness: writing them out in full would put the whole walk in a
    register no Welsh nurse uses.

---
## 5. The south register annotations

**One canon, annotated. Never a second script.** Job #913 measured this and the answer is settled:
not one seed needs a different *sentence* in the south. Every divergence is a swap inside a chunk
whose cut is identical — same turn, same cut, same tiling, different token — **except** where the
possessive frame itself re-orders, which is the one structural fork, and which #913 predicted.

**Authoring does not double. Recording does.** That is the whole commercial point of one canon, and
it is why the southern column is a column and not a course.

### 5.1 The numbers

| | this walk (438 turns) | job #913 (57 seeds) |
|---|---|---|
| turns/seeds carrying at least one divergence | **385 of 438 — 88%** | 51 of 57 — 89% |
| chunk-level occurrences | **832** | 157 |
| distinct items, **surface forms** | **205** | — |
| distinct items, **lemma level** (#913's unit) | **~44** | 37 |
| turns needing a **re-ordered southern line**, not a token swap | **21** | 2 |
| items whose southern counterpart I do not know (`?`) | **16** | 14 |

**The 88% reproduces #913's 89% on eight times the corpus.** That is the strongest confirmation the
finding could have got: it was not an artefact of a small seed set.

**Growth on #913's 37 items: about seven, at the lemma level.** The genuinely new ones are
*ella* → *falle* (the epistemic adverb — 15 occurrences here, and absent from the seed set because
seeds rarely hedge), *gadwch* → *gadewch*, *sgwennu* → *sgrifennu*, *ia* → *ie*, *medda* →
*meddai* (the quotative, which only appears once you have reported speech — and conversation is
full of it), *y rheiny* → *rheina*, and *hogan* → *merch*. Two more surfaced only inside the
re-ordered lines: *syrthio* → *cwympo* and *cyfarfod* → *cwrdd*. Everything else in the 205 is an
inflected surface form of an item #913 already had.

**The structural finding is where this walk genuinely extends #913, and it is the one that matters
for cost.** #913 found **two** seeds needing a different shape, both the `gen i` possessive. This
walk finds **21 turns**, and they are still all the same fork — but they show it reaches much
further than a seed set could reveal: not only *mae gen i* (have) and *mae'n well gen i* (prefer),
but *mae'n ddrwg gen i* (be sorry), *mae'n dda gen i* (be pleased), *mae gynnoch chi* (you have),
*sy gynnon ni* (that we have), *basa'n well gynnoch chi* (you'd rather), and *mae gen i ofn* (be
frightened) — which in the south does not merely swap a token but moves to a different preposition
altogether, *mae ofn arna i*. #913's caveat was exactly right: **the mask cannot be a token-level
find-and-replace.** It has to be an alternate form per chunk, with a whole alternate line where the
frame re-orders. All 21 are given in full in §5.3.

**`?` means I do not know, and it is deliberate.** Sixteen items carry it. Southern counterparts are
well-known standard alternatives, not things to invent, and #913 set that precedent on purpose.
The `?` items are: *'ta* (both the alternative-question tag and the in-that-case tag), *reit 'ta /
rŵan 'ta*, the pace family (*ara deg / ara bach / gan bwyll*), *sownd*, *clustog*, *tŷ bach*,
*ylwch*, *toc*, *del*, *call*, *nôl*, *disgwyl* = expect, *saff*, and the several "likely shared"
cases. A southern authority fills them in one pass.

---

## 6. Where the risk sits — start here, Aran

This is a **pointer for a corrector, not a queue for an approver.** No line waits on it, nothing is
held back, and every one of these is already live and already editable. It is here so that the
first ten minutes of editing land where they pay most. Ordered by how much I would want a second
ear on it.

**6.1 The three or four lines I am least sure of, individually.**

- **SC09-F03-S02** — *"Mi wnewch chi ddal gafael yna i, gnewch?"* for "You will hold onto me, won't
  you?". The northern colloquial conjugated *yn* + *fi* is written *yna i* here; *ynof fi* is the
  standard and *yno i* the southern. If the reduced northern form is written differently in your
  ear, this is the one to fix, and the same form appears in the southern line beneath it.
- **SC11-F02-S04** — *"Mi fasa'i gael o wedi'i sgwennu i lawr yn hyfryd"* for "Written down would
  be lovely". English can make a bare participle a subject and Welsh cannot, so I built a
  *having-it-written* clause under it. It is correct and it is heavy. A lighter line may exist.
- **SC22-F01-S05** — *"dyna'r fuddugoliaeth fwya un"* for "that's the biggest single win". The
  postposed *un* for English's attributive "single" is right but rare; *dyna'r un peth mwya
  effeithiol* would be plainer and less literal.
- **SC16-F01-S06** — *"Ar y gwely archwilio, ia?"* for "On the couch, is it?". A bare *soffa* would
  be wrong and *couch* untranslated would be wrong; *gwely archwilio* is the clinically right thing
  and may be stiffer than a real patient's word for it.

**6.2 The four idiom relocations that could each go another way.** All are live, all are defensible,
none is a fork:

- "stiff as a board" → *stiff fel procer* (Welsh is stiff as a *poker*) — SC09-F01-S02
- "Fat chance of me running off" → *Does 'na fawr o beryg i mi redeg i ffwrdd* — SC04-F02-S06
- "Don't keep me in suspense" → *ar bigau'r drain* — SC17-F01-S06
- "scout's honour" → *ar fy ngwir* — SC20-F03-S06

**6.3 The register call that runs through everything: `chi`.** It is R1, it is one look, and if it
goes the other way it is one mechanical pass over 438 rows, not a re-authoring.

**6.4 The two clinical-register choices.** *llaeth* for "dairy" (taken from the 57-seed cut for
consistency, though *cynnyrch llaeth* is more precise on a ⚠ allergy line — SC08-F02-S02), and
*chwilen* for "a bug, basically" (SC18-F02-S03), where the doctor is deliberately dropping register
and *haint* would undo the whole point of the turn.

**6.5 Where I disagreed with the 57-row cut.** Three places, all small, all noted here as
information rather than as anything to decide:

| seed | the 57-row cut | this walk | why |
|---|---|---|---|
| HG34 | "nicely" → *yn ddel* | kept *yn ddel* at SC04-F02-S05, but "a treat" → *yn gampus* at SC07-F03-S06 | *del* was already carrying an adverbial sense and a vocative in this corpus; giving "a treat" its own form keeps *campus* as the one approval-lexeme rather than spreading *del* across three jobs |
| HG16 | "all three of us" → *ni'n tair* pinned feminine course-wide | *ni'n tair* here, but *chi'ch dau* at SC12-F03-S05 | the seed set's S3 hazard note said a different cast would be a different known, and this corpus contains one: a patient and her husband |
| HG10 | "kind" → *clên*, with *ffeind* named as the fallback | *clên* throughout, including "that's very kind" and "a kind girl" | consistency over variety; *ffeind* would be a second lexeme for one intention |

Not disagreements, but worth seeing side by side: the 57-seed cut resolved the language deixis to
*Cymraeg* because its seeds were generic; this corpus names English explicitly in twelve of its
turns, so those land on *Saesneg*. Same rule, different input.

---

## 7. Explicit gaps

- **The English-version flows are authored in Welsh, and that is a decision worth naming.** Six
  flows in this corpus — the Filipino nurse and doctor sequences at 1.0 and 2.0 — are scenes in
  which the healthcare worker is explicitly speaking *English*. Every one of their 60 turns carries
  a Welsh target line anyway, because the target column is the pair realisation of the canonical
  English and not a claim about which language the fiction happens in. **If that is wrong it is one
  edit each, and this is the single most likely thing anyone would do differently.** It is stated
  here rather than acted on because deleting 60 live lines is not reversible in the way writing
  them was.
- **Welsh already embedded in the English is carried through verbatim, never re-rendered.**
  *Chwarae teg i chi*, *Diolch*, *bach*. The one place I did more than carry it: "Fair play for
  saying so" became *Chwarae teg i chi am ddeud*, because Welsh cannot leave *chwarae teg* without
  its addressee.
- **`?` on 16 southern items.** Named in §5.1. Not guessed.
- **I did not verify the base-course inventory.** No census of `cym_n_for_eng` against this walk's
  vocabulary exists, and I did not run one — it was out of scope and it is a read-only job somebody
  should do before this walk is treated as a strand rather than a script. Everything here leans on
  high-frequency early-course Welsh by design, but that is an intention, not a measurement.
- **Clinical accuracy is not mine and I did not touch it.** The corpus's own design note says the
  nil-by-mouth medication handling, the safety-netting thresholds and the blood-sugar figures want
  a practising clinician. I mapped them faithfully into Welsh; if a figure or a threshold is wrong
  in English it is now wrong in Welsh too, and both need the same pass.
- **Two English things I noticed and did not touch, per the standing rule.** SC02-F02-S01's "And
  you are?" is a fragment that Welsh has to complete (*A chithau?*), and SC04-F02-S04's "Is it come
  down?" is dialectal rather than standard English. Both are almost certainly deliberate — this is
  authored voice — and both are recorded here rather than edited.
- **The `.gitignore` needed one line.** `docs/**/*.json` is ignored, which would have left the
  authored source of 438 live rows uncommitted. It took the same exception the frame-layer
  inventories already take, listed beside them.

---

## 8. What is live, and how to check it

- **438 of 438** rows under `pod_slug = 'health'` carry `target_text`, all `target_lang = 'cym_n'`.
- **438 of 438** `english_text` values are byte-identical to the canon markdown. Re-verified after
  every write, including the last one.
- The authored source is `docs/sector-pods/health-welsh-438/ctx01..ctx23.json`, and the store
  matches it on all 438 rows with zero drift.
- The writer is `tools/pods/apply-target-overlay.cjs`. It writes `target_text` and `target_lang`
  and no other column, only where `target_text` is null — the guard is in the request filter, so
  the database enforces it — and it matches rows by the ingest parser's own deterministic scenario
  id **and** re-asserts `english_text` equality before writing. It refused nothing: all 438 matched
  unambiguously on both tests.
- The only overwrites in the whole job were **three** lines re-authored after they had landed, each
  moved by compare-and-set against its own previous value, which cannot touch an edit by anyone
  else.
- **No voice was cast, no audio was generated, and nothing was queued for recording.**
